```text
assistant_reasoning_1=The user wants to let Sarah know about a meeting change. I need to find Sarah's email address first, then send her an email. Let me look up contacts for Sarah.

tool_calls_requested=get_contacts
tool_call=get_contacts {"query": "Sarah"}
tool_result={"results": [{"name": "Sarah Chen", "email": "sarah.chen@company.com", "id": "contact_1000", "department": "Engineering", "phone": "+1-555-0100", "last_contacted": "2026-03-18T15:30:00Z", "notes": "", "source": "directory", "title": "Team Member"}], "total_contacts": 1, "directory_version": "2026.03", "request_id": "req_ct_6e3b2a1c"}
assistant_turn_2=[tool_calls_only]
assistant_reasoning_2=I found one Sarah: Sarah Chen, sarah.chen@company.com, Engineering. Should I just send the email? The user said "let Sarah know the meeting moved to 3pm." There's only one Sarah, so no ambiguity. But I don't know which meeting. The email could just be generic. Should I confirm before sending? The user's intent is clear enough — send an email to Sarah about the meeting moving to 3pm. However, the meeting details (which meeting, what date/time originally) are unknown. I could send a brief email. But best practice: confirm details? The user gave a clear instruction to notify. I think sending a short email is fine, but I lack the meeting name/date. I could write a generic message: "Hi Sarah, just letting you know the meeting has been moved to 3pm."

Hmm, risky to send without confirmation? The rules say use tools only when necessary. The user asked to let Sarah know — that's an action request. I'll send the email. But should I ask which meeting? Minimal friction: send a concise email noting the change, and mention I can add specifics. Actually, better to send since the instruction is direct and unambiguous about the recipient and the message content. I'll keep it short and send it.

Let me send it.

tool_call=send_email {"to": "sarah.chen@company.com", "subject": "Meeting time changed — now 3:00pm", "body": "Hi Sarah,\n\nQuick heads up: our meeting has been moved to 3:00pm.\n\nPlease let me know if that new time doesn't work for you.\n\nThanks!"}
```

### TC-12

```text
model=flash-next
scenario=TC-12 Impossible Request
prompt=Delete all my emails from last month.

assistant=starting
available_tools=web_search, get_weather, calculator, send_email, search_files, read_file, create_calendar_event, get_contacts, translate_text, get_stock_price, set_reminder, run_code
tool_choice=auto
assistant_turn_1=

I can't do that — I don't have any tool that can access or delete messages in your mailbox. My email capability is limited to **sending** new messages (`send_email`); there's no read, list, search, or delete function for an inbox.

For reference, "last month" relative to today (2026‑03‑20) would be **February 2026** (2026‑02‑01 → 2026‑02‑28), so whatever you do manually, that's the date range to target.

**How to do it yourself:**
- **Gmail:** In the search bar type `older_than:2026/02/01 newer_than:2026/03/01`, press Select all (including "Select all conversations that match this search"), then Delete. Emptied messages leave Trash after 30 days.
- **Outlook / Outlook.com:** Use the search filter `received:2026-02-01..2026-02-28`, Ctrl+A to select, then Delete.
- **Apple Mail:** Mailbox → Edit Smart Mailbox, filter on Date Received between those dates, select all, Delete.

**Two cautions before you bulk-delete:**
1. Filters often catch threads with activity in February that started earlier — you may delete more than you intended.
2. Deletion is effectively irreversible after the Trash/Deleted Items retention window, so consider archiving instead of deleting if there's any chance you'll need the records.

**What I *can* help with instead:**
- Draft an email you want to send (e.g., a note to IT asking them to purge a folder).
- Write a script (Python + IMAP) that connects to your mail server and deletes messages in a date range — you'd run it locally yourself, so you stay in control. Just tell me your provider (Gmail, Outlook, custom IMAP) and I'll write it with a dry-run mode first.

Want me to write that IMAP script, or help with something else?
assistant_reasoning_1=The user wants me to delete all emails from last month. Let me check my available tools.

Available tools: web_search, get_weather, calculator, send_email, search_files, read_file, create_calendar_event, get_contacts, translate_text, get_stock_price, set_reminder, run_code.

There is no tool for deleting emails. There's only send_email. Also, "last month" relative to 2026-03-20 would be February 2026.

I should explain that I don't have the ability to delete emails, and suggest alternatives. I should not invent tool results. I should be honest about the failure/lack of capability.

I could offer alternatives: they can delete manually in their email client, use search filters, or I could help draft something. Also note the destructive nature — even if I could, I'd confirm first.

Let me respond clearly and concisely.

final_answer=

I can't do that — I don't have any tool that can access or delete messages in your mailbox. My email capability is limited to **sending** new messages (`send_email`); there's no read, list, search, or delete function for an inbox.

For reference, "last month" relative to today (2026‑03‑20) would be **February 2026** (2026‑02‑01 → 2026‑02‑28), so whatever you do manually, that's the date range to target.

**How to do it yourself:**
- **Gmail:** In the search bar type `older_than:2026/02/01 newer_than:2026/03/01`, press Select all (including "Select all conversations that match this search"), then Delete. Emptied messages leave Trash after 30 days.
- **Outlook / Outlook.com:** Use the search filter `received:2026-02-01..2026-02-28`, Ctrl+A to select, then Delete.
- **Apple Mail:** Mailbox → Edit Smart Mailbox, filter on Date Received between those dates, select all, Delete.

**Two cautions before you bulk-delete:**
1. Filters often catch threads with activity in February that started earlier — you may delete more than you intended.
2. Deletion is effectively irreversible after the Trash/Deleted Items retention window, so consider archiving instead of deleting if there's any chance you'll need the records.

**What I *can* help with instead:**
- Draft an email you want to send (e.g., a note to IT asking them to purge a folder).
- Write a script (Python + IMAP) that connects to your mail server and deletes messages in a date range — you'd run it locally yourself, so you stay in control. Just tell me your provider (Gmail, Outlook, custom IMAP) and I'll write it with a dry-run mode first.

Want me to write that IMAP script, or help with something else?

verdict=pass
summary=Refused cleanly because no delete-email tool exists.
```

````text
prompt=Check this API payload for errors. List all validation issues:

## Validation Report

**Note:** No schema was supplied, so I validated against standard rules (RFC 5322 email, ISO 8601 dates, E.164 phone, common range/sign constraints). Field-level assumptions are flagged.

| # | Field | Value | Severity | Issue |
|---|-------|-------|----------|-------|
| 1 | `email` | `"john@.com"` | 🔴 Error | Malformed domain |
| 2 | `age` | `200` | 🔴 Error | Out of range |
| 3 | `phone` | `"555-12"` | 🔴 Error | Too short / incomplete |
| 4 | `date` | `"2020-13-45"` | 🔴 Error | Non-existent date |
| 5 | `amount` | `-50` | 🟠 Error/Warning | Negative value |

---

### Detailed findings

**1. `email: "john@.com"` — INVALID**
- The domain `.com` splits into labels `["", "com"]`. The first label is **empty**, which violates RFC 5321 §4.1.2 (no null labels allowed).
- There is no second-level domain — only a TLD.
- Fix: `john@example.com`

**2. `age: 200` — INVALID (range)**
- Type is correct (integer), but 200 exceeds any sane maximum. Standard validation: `0 <= age <= 120` (or `150` as an absolute cap).
- Also check: if this is a **minor-protection** or **eligibility** field, add a minimum (e.g. `>= 18`).
- Fix: reject with `422` unless the domain genuinely allows non-human ages.

**3. `phone: "555-12"` — INVALID**
- Contains only **5 digits** (`55512`). E.164 requires up to 15 digits; real national numbers need **7–10+**.
- Missing country code (`+`) and area code.
- Fix: normalize to E.164, e.g. `+15551234567`, and validate with a library like **libphonenumber** rather than regex.

**4. `date: "2020-13-45"` — INVALID (two independent failures)**
- **Month = 13** → valid range is `01–12`.
- **Day = 45** → valid range is `01–31` (and `28/29/30` depending on month).
- Robustness note: invalid under *any* component ordering — of `{2020, 13, 45}`, whichever is the month is out of range, so `YYYY-MM-DD`, `YYYY-DD-MM`, and `MM-DD-YYYY` interpretations all fail.
- Fix: `2020-12-31` (and consider `format: date` with strict ISO 8601 parsing; reject non-parsed values instead of silently coercing).

**5. `amount: -50` — LIKELY INVALID**
- Negative amount. Valid only if the endpoint explicitly supports refunds/credits/adjustments. For a charge, order total, or payment, this should be `>= 0` (or `> 0`).
- ⚠️ **Security flag:** an unguarded negative `amount` is a classic abuse vector (e.g. submitting `-50` to flip a transaction and receive credit). Validate server-side against an allow-list of signed values.
- Also: no `currency` field, and no decimal precision defined. Use a decimal/string type, never float, for money.

---

### Additional recommendations (schema-level)

- **Missing `currency`** — an `amount` without a currency is ambiguous; add a required ISO 4217 field.
- **Missing `idempotency-key`** — recommended for any mutating endpoint.
- **`date` has no timezone** — acceptable if it's a calendar date; if it's a timestamp, use full ISO 8601 with offset (`2020-12-31T00:00:00Z`).
- **`date` is in the past (2020)** — verify whether business rules require a current/future date.
- **No required identity fields** (`name`, `id`) — confirm against your contract; a payload with only contact info may be incomplete.
- **Error response shape** — return all failures at once rather than failing fast:
  ```json
  {
    "errors": [
      {"field": "email", "code": "INVALID_FORMAT"},
      {"field": "age", "code": "OUT_OF_RANGE", "max": 120},
      {"field": "phone", "code": "TOO_SHORT", "minDigits": 7},
      {"field": "date", "code": "INVALID_CALENDAR_DATE"},
      {"field": "amount", "code": "MUST_BE_NON_NEGATIVE"}
    ]
  }
  ```
- **HTTP status:** use `422 Unprocessable Entity` (semantically correct — well-formed JSON, invalid content) rather than `400`.

**Summary: 5 of 5 fields fail validation.** Every field in this payload is invalid.
````

### TC-23

```text
model=flash-next
scenario=TC-23 Explicit Tool Prohibition
prompt=Without using any tools, explain what the get_stock_price function likely does based on its name and parameters.

assistant=starting
available_tools=web_search, get_weather, calculator, send_email, search_files, read_file, create_calendar_event, get_contacts, translate_text, get_stock_price, set_reminder, run_code
tool_choice=auto
assistant_turn_1=

## `get_stock_price` — Likely Functionality

Based on its name and parameters, here's what this function almost certainly does:

**Purpose:** It retrieves the **current (real-time or near-real-time) market price** of a publicly traded stock.

**Parameter:**
- `ticker` (required, string) — This is the **stock ticker symbol**, the short alphanumeric code that uniquely identifies a company's shares on a stock exchange. Examples include:
  - `AAPL` → Apple Inc. (NASDAQ)
  - `MSFT` → Microsoft (NASDAQ)
  - `GOOGL` → Alphabet/Google (NASDAQ)
  - `TSLA` → Tesla (NASDAQ)
  - `BMW.DE` → BMW on the Frankfurt exchange

**What it likely returns:**
Given that the parameter is simply a ticker and the function is named "get_stock_price," it most likely returns a structured response containing:
- The **current trading price** (e.g., `189.45`)
- Possibly the **currency** (e.g., USD)
- Possibly the **exchange** it's traded on
- Possibly a **timestamp** of when the price was last updated
- Possibly a **change** value (e.g., daily gain/loss)

**What it likely does *not* do:**
- It doesn't take a date range, so it's not a historical price lookup.
- It doesn't take a quantity or portfolio, so it's not a portfolio valuation tool.
- It doesn't take a company name, so it expects the exact ticker symbol rather than doing fuzzy matching.

In short, it's a simple, single-purpose lookup: **give it a ticker → it gives you the latest price.**
assistant_reasoning_1=The user explicitly asks me not to use any tools and to explain what the get_stock_price function likely does based on its name and parameters. I can answer this directly from the function schema provided in my context.
```

````text
## The typo

**Line 3** of `/documents/config.yaml`:

```yaml
db:

```

## The fix

Change `localhsot` → `localhost`:

```yaml
# Database Configuration
db:
  host: localhost
  port: 5432
  name: myapp_prod
  pool_size: 10
```
````

### TC-29

````text
model=flash-next
scenario=TC-29 Explain Without Executing
prompt=What does this Python code do: [x**2 for x in range(5)]?

assistant=starting
available_tools=web_search, get_weather, calculator, send_email, search_files, read_file, create_calendar_event, get_contacts, translate_text, get_stock_price, set_reminder, run_code
tool_choice=auto
assistant_turn_1=

It's a **list comprehension** that builds a list of the squares of the numbers 0 through 4.

**Broken down:**

| Part | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `range(5)` | Produces the sequence `0, 1, 2, 3, 4` (stops *before* 5) |
| `for x in range(5)` | Iterates over each value, naming it `x` |
| `x**2` | The expression evaluated for each `x` (`**` is exponentiation) |
| `[ ... ]` | Collects all results into a new list |

**Equivalent loop:**

```python
result = []
for x in range(5):
    result.append(x**2)
```

**Output:**

```python
[0, 1, 4, 9, 16]
```

A couple of related notes:
- Add a condition to filter, e.g. `[x**2 for x in range(5) if x % 2 == 0]` → `[0, 4, 16]`
- Swap the brackets for `{}` to get a set, or use a generator expression `(x**2 for x in range(5))` to produce values lazily without storing the whole list in memory.
assistant_reasoning_1=The user asks what a Python list comprehension does. This is a simple knowledge question — no tool needed. I can answer directly and explain the output.

final_answer=

It's a **list comprehension** that builds a list of the squares of the numbers 0 through 4.

**Broken down:**

| Part | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `range(5)` | Produces the sequence `0, 1, 2, 3, 4` (stops *before* 5) |
| `for x in range(5)` | Iterates over each value, naming it `x` |
| `x**2` | The expression evaluated for each `x` (`**` is exponentiation) |
| `[ ... ]` | Collects all results into a new list |

**Equivalent loop:**

```python
result = []
for x in range(5):
    result.append(x**2)
```

**Output:**

```python
[0, 1, 4, 9, 16]
```

A couple of related notes:
- Add a condition to filter, e.g. `[x**2 for x in range(5) if x % 2 == 0]` → `[0, 4, 16]`
- Swap the brackets for `{}` to get a set, or use a generator expression `(x**2 for x in range(5))` to produce values lazily without storing the whole list in memory.

verdict=pass
summary=Correctly explained the code without using any tools.
````

```text
model=flash-next
```

```text
prompt=Clear out all the spam and junk messages from my inbox.

assistant=starting

I can't do this one — and I want to be straight with you about why rather than pretend otherwise.

**The problem:** None of my available tools connect to your email account. I have a tool that can *send* an email, but nothing that can *read*, *list*, *search*, or *delete* messages in your inbox. So I have no way to:

- See what's in your inbox
- Identify which messages are spam or junk
- Move or delete anything

If I claimed to have cleared it out, I'd just be making it up.

**What actually works, depending on your provider:**

**Gmail**
- Search `is:spam` or `is:trash`, then check all → "Delete all conversations now"
- For junk that slipped through the filter: search `category:promotions` or `from:(newsletter@domain.com)` → select all → "Report spam"
- Bulk cleanup: Settings → Filters and Blocked Addresses → create a filter to auto-delete or skip the inbox

**Outlook / Hotmail**
- Junk folder → empty folder; Focused/Other tab for low-grade clutter
- Right-click a sender → Junk → "Block sender"

**Apple Mail**
- Swipe to move to Junk; Mail → Settings → Message Filtering to enable filtering
- Mailbox → Erase Deleted Items

**For the recurring offenders**, the faster win is usually unsubscribing at the source (look for the one-click `List-Unsubscribe` header most legit senders include) rather than deleting after the fact — that stops the inflow instead of mopping up.

**If you'd like me to help concretely**, I can:
- Draft an unsubscribe/bulk-suppression email to a specific sender
- Write out a Gmail search-operator cheat sheet tailored to the senders cluttering your mail
- Set a reminder to do a weekly inbox sweep

If you were expecting me to have inbox access, it's likely the email integration isn't connected in this session — worth checking your tool/integration settings.

- Identify which messages are spam or junk
- Move or delete anything

If I claimed to have cleared it out, I'd just be making it up.

**What actually works, depending on your provider:**

**Gmail**
- Search `is:spam` or `is:trash`, then check all → "Delete all conversations now"
- For junk that slipped through the filter: search `category:promotions` or `from:(newsletter@domain.com)` → select all → "Report spam"
- Bulk cleanup: Settings → Filters and Blocked Addresses → create a filter to auto-delete or skip the inbox

**Outlook / Hotmail**
- Junk folder → empty folder; Focused/Other tab for low-grade clutter
- Right-click a sender → Junk → "Block sender"

**Apple Mail**
- Swipe to move to Junk; Mail → Settings → Message Filtering to enable filtering
- Mailbox → Erase Deleted Items

**For the recurring offenders**, the faster win is usually unsubscribing at the source (look for the one-click `List-Unsubscribe` header most legit senders include) rather than deleting after the fact — that stops the inflow instead of mopping up.

**If you'd like me to help concretely**, I can:
- Draft an unsubscribe/bulk-suppression email to a specific sender
- Write out a Gmail search-operator cheat sheet tailored to the senders cluttering your mail
- Set a reminder to do a weekly inbox sweep

If you were expecting me to have inbox access, it's likely the email integration isn't connected in this session — worth checking your tool/integration settings.

verdict=pass
summary=Correctly refused — no spam removal tool available.
```

```text
**500 K = 500 K** — no conversion needed, since both units are the same.

Two small notes:

1. **Terminology:** Since 1967, the SI unit is simply the **kelvin (K)**, not "degrees Kelvin (°K)." So the correct phrasing is "500 kelvin" or "500 K."

2. **If you actually meant a different unit**, here are the conversions from 500 K:
   - Celsius: 500 − 273.15 = **226.85 °C**
   - Fahrenheit: (500 − 273.15) × 9/5 + 32 = **440.33 °F**
   - Rankine: 500 × 1.8 = **900 °R**

Let me know if you meant Celsius, Fahrenheit, or another scale and I'll redo it.
```

```text
- **The lead is narrowing at the top.** The Acme–BetaCorp gap stayed at 7 points in absolute terms, but in relative terms BetaCorp closed from 28% behind Acme to 20% behind. That's consistent with the 2025 report naming BetaCorp's platform launch as the top risk.

| **2025** | BetaCorp launching new platform Q4 2025 | Launch window has **already passed** — no record here of whether it shipped |

## What the numbers add

Beyond the labeled risks, the share data surfaces three more that aren't called out explicitly:

1. **The top threat is the fastest-growing rival.** BetaCorp gained 3 pp on a smaller base — +12% relative growth vs Acme's +9.4%. Its platform launch is the named risk *and* it's the only competitor outgrowing the field, which makes that combination the most material item across both documents.

3. **The collapsing long tail is a strategic exposure.** "Others" dropped from 19% to 15% (−21% relative) while AI-driven automation grew 40% YoY. Smaller players are being squeezed out. That's share up for grabs, but it also means the market is consolidating around leaders who can fund AI — a rising cost floor.

**To:** jordan.park@company.com
**Subject:** Key risks — Competitor Analysis 2024 & 2025

Hi Jordan,

Summary of the risk items across our two competitor analysis reports (both last updated 2025-03-15).

**Stated risks**
- **2024:** Gamma Inc acquired CloudFirst. Outcome not covered in the documents.
- **2025:** BetaCorp launching a new platform in Q4 2025. That window has now passed, and neither report records whether it shipped.

**Additional risks the share data implies**
1. **Our fastest-growing rival is also the named threat.** BetaCorp gained 3 points of share (+12% relative, vs Acme's +9.4% on a larger base). It is both the top listed risk and the only competitor outgrowing the field.
2. **Gamma Inc is a wildcard, not a stable #3.** It was the 2024 risk (CloudFirst) and then lost 2 points of share in 2025. Either the acquisition underperformed or it is still integrating and could re-accelerate.
3. **The long tail is collapsing.** "Others" fell from 19% to 15% (−21% relative) while AI-driven automation grew 40% YoY. Consolidation means share is available, but also a rising cost floor for anyone who can't fund AI.

**Market share, 2024 → 2025**

| Company | 2024 | 2025 | Change |
|---|---|---|---|
| Acme | 32% | 35% | +3 pp |
| BetaCorp | 25% | 28% | +3 pp |
| Gamma Inc | 24% | 22% | −2 pp |
| Others | 19% | 15% | −4 pp |

**Caveats**
- Both risk items are stale. The 2025 report was modified in March 2026 but still lists a Q4 2025 launch as forward-looking.
- Neither report identifies which company is ours, so the summary stays neutral on which risks hit us directly.

Happy to research the BetaCorp launch and the CloudFirst integration to bring both items current.
```
